# Backlog

Epics map to milestones in `docs/design.md`. An epic is `planned` until `/next-epic` activates it, then `active` until every story is checked, then `done`. `/next-story` takes the first unchecked story of the active epic.

Stories under a `planned` epic are drafts. `/next-epic` re-checks them against `docs/design.md` and what earlier epics taught us, rewrites what no longer holds, then activates the epic.

## Story format

```
- [ ] E<n>-S<m> Imperative title. One-line goal.
  - Accept: what a person can see in the game, or a test can assert.
  - Test: named GUT file, or how to verify by hand.
  - Touches: folders.
  - Settles: open items the story must close first (spike stories only).
```

Stories name systems by folder. They name a file only when it is a test, or when a skill already expects that exact path. A story touching more than two folders gets split.

## E1: Ball on a tile — done

Ships as 0.1.0. Retires the biggest risk: the isometric projection and the height map fighting each other in the physics.

Done when: you can paint hills, fire a debug shot over them, and the ball lands where the math said it would.

- [x] E1-S1 Stand up the project skeleton. Get a repo, a main scene with Build and Play states, and green checks.
  - Accept: `git init` done with a first commit, since `/next-story` commits and this folder is not yet a repo. Pick the engine version and record it with `/decision`: the repo says 4.3 and `scripts/install_godot.sh` pins it, but `godot` on this machine is 4.7.2. GUT is pinned to a release that runs on that version and installed in `addons/gut`. `scenes/main.tscn` exists (`project.godot` already points at it) and a key toggles Build and Play with no scene load. `.gitignore` is reviewed: it currently ignores `*.import`, which loses per-asset import settings on a fresh clone.
  - Test: `scripts/run_tests.sh` exits 0 with one real test in `tests/test_main_states.gd`. It exits 3 without GUT, so GUT is part of this story.
  - Touches: `scenes/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`
- [x] E1-S2 Build the CourseData resource. Give every system one source of truth.
  - Accept: a 2D tile array (height 0 to 7, surface enum), a holes list, and `to_dict` / `from_dict` in course format version 1 from `/course-file`, including the base64 `tiles` packing and `oob` as surface 8. A 20x20 course round-trips through JSON with no loss. Out-of-range heights are rejected on load.
  - Test: `tests/test_course_data.gd`
  - Touches: `course/`, `tests/`
- [x] E1-S3 Build the placeholder art and screenshot tools. `/art-spec` and `/next-story` both call tools that do not exist yet.
  - Accept: `art/palette.gpl` (about 32 colors), `scripts/placeholder.py`, `scripts/screenshot.sh <scene>`, and specs plus placeholders for surfaces and risers. Each surface has its colorblind pattern from day one. `screenshot.sh` writes a real PNG: Godot's `--headless` mode has a dummy renderer and draws no pixels, so it needs a display (`xvfb-run` is not installed on this machine).
  - Test: by hand. Run the screenshot script on a test scene and open the PNG.
  - Touches: `art/`, `scripts/`
- [x] E1-S4 Render the course isometrically. Draw any CourseData with heights correct and a usable camera.
  - Accept: TileMapLayer draws surfaces, risers per height step, Y-sort on. A 20x20 course with heights 0 to 7 has no overlap artifacts at 0.5x, 1x and 2x. Pan with middle mouse or WASD. Tile-to-screen and screen-to-tile projection live in one shared helper.
  - Test: `tests/test_iso_projection.gd` round-trips tile to screen to tile at every height. Screenshot at each zoom.
  - Touches: `course/`, `scenes/`, `tests/`
- [x] E1-S5 Paint terrain. Let the player shape land through the builder, the only writer.
  - Accept: surface palette for the 8 surfaces, raise and lower, brush 1/3/5, undo stack. The tile under the cursor is picked correctly on raised ground, including a tile partly hidden by a nearer hill. 50 edits then 50 undos restore the original CourseData hash. Heights clamp at 0 and 7.
  - Test: `tests/test_builder.gd`
  - Touches: `course/`, `scenes/`, `tests/`
- [x] E1-S6 Step the ball over the height map. Custom 3D physics at 60 Hz, no scene needed, so guests can reuse it.
  - Accept: gravity, one bounce by surface, roll downhill with surface friction read from a resource, height interpolated between tiles. A flying ball that meets a hillside lands there. Randomness comes from a seeded generator owned by the ball, never the global one. The same seed and inputs give the identical landing position 100 of 100 runs, not just the same tile.
  - Test: `tests/test_ball.gd`
  - Touches: `golf/`, `tests/`
  - Settles: yards per height step, gravity and bounce values (none are in `docs/design.md` yet; add them there).
- [x] E1-S7 Fire a debug shot. See the physics on the projected map.
  - Accept: a button fires the ball at fixed power and angle. The sprite follows the projected path and a ground shadow shows its height. The ball lands on the predicted tile, within 1 tile, on flat and sloped terrain. The prediction comes from running the same step function ahead of time.
  - Test: `tests/test_shot_prediction.gd` covers flat and sloped courses. Screenshot mid-flight.
  - Touches: `golf/`, `scenes/`, `tests/`
- [x] E1-S8 Make `/ship-it` runnable. Its smoke test and export setup do not exist yet.
  - Accept: `scripts/playtest.gd` runs headless and asserts only what the current milestone has built. For E1 that is firing a shot and landing it. `/playtest` smoke is updated to say so: its full bar (holed out, guest reaction, money changed) cannot pass before E4, yet `/ship-it` requires it at every milestone. `docs/export.md` and `export_presets.cfg` exist for Linux and Windows.
  - Test: run smoke headless. Export a Linux build and launch it. Export templates must be installed first.
  - Touches: `scripts/`, `docs/`
- [x] E1-S9 Decide grid size and height model. Close the two open questions the design doc assigns to this milestone.
  - Accept: paint a 3-step green contour and a hillside, fire shots across them, take screenshots, then record two `/decision` entries. They answer whether 5 yards per tile is too coarse for green contours and whether sub-step ramps are needed. The design doc already assumes ramp tiles, so the decision states what "ramp" means. If either answer changes the data model, file a story here before closing the epic.
  - Test: screenshots attached to the decisions in `docs/DECISIONS.md`. `/godot-check` green.
  - Touches: `docs/`
  - Settles: design doc open questions "Grid size" and "Height model".
- [x] E1-S10 Show rises that face away from the camera. Make every slope visible without camera rotation.
  - Note: found by E1-S9 (D5). Risers are drawn only on faces that point at the camera. A rise along +x or +y shows no riser and identical tops, so the 3-step green contour and the 7-step hillside in `docs/decisions/e1-s9-green.png` and `e1-s9-hill.png` look flat. No data model change.
  - Accept: a tile higher than its back neighbour (x - 1 or y - 1) draws an edge cue along that back edge, because that rise faces away from the camera and has no riser. A tile whose back neighbour is higher draws no cue there, since the neighbour's riser already shows it (`CourseView.riser_frame` draws faces toward x + 1 and y + 1). The cue uses palette colors, has a pattern as well as a color, and differs by step count, or at least marks a step of one versus more. Every step of `GridHeightEvidence.green_contour()` and `hillside()` can be counted at 0.5x, 1x and 2x. The cue depends on the back neighbours, so `refresh_cell` also redraws (x + 1, y) and (x, y + 1), and editing a tile's height updates the cues in front of it. Picking and draw order are unchanged.
  - Test: cases in `tests/test_course_view.gd` check that the cue is present on a tile higher than its back neighbour, absent on a flat tile, and absent where the back neighbour is higher. A further case raises a tile and checks that `refresh_cell` adds the cue on its front neighbours. Screenshots: `EVIDENCE_CASE=green` and `EVIDENCE_CASE=hill scripts/screenshot.sh tests/fixtures/grid_height_shot.tscn`.
  - Touches: `course/`, `art/`, `tests/`
- [x] E1-S11 Make green contours putt as breaks. A one-step contour on a green should bend a putt, not wall it off.
  - Note: found by E1-S9 (D5). One step is a 40% slope. On a contoured green, 7 to 9 yd/s uphill putts finish within 0.7 yd of where they started, and a sidehill putt falls 5 yd sideways (`docs/decisions/e1-s9-probe.txt`). No data model change. This could move to E2, before E2-S5, at `/next-epic`.
  - Accept: a per-surface `slope_scale` in `course/surfaces.tres` (green about 0.075, every other surface 1.0) scales the slope pull on a rolling ball and the tilt of the ground normal at touchdown on that surface. The ball's height still follows the terrain. Scaling the pull alone breaks the rest rule: on green a scaled one-step pull is about 10.7 x 0.03 = 0.32 yd/s², below the constant friction `roll_decel` / roll = 1.5 / 1.4 = 1.07 yd/s², so a still or slow ball on a green slope would never start downhill. So the constant friction gets a per-surface value too (for example a `roll_decel` column, or the global one scaled by `slope_scale`), set so the scaled one-step pull beats it on every rolling surface, and the green's drag is retuned so a 4 yd/s putt still runs about 3.7 yd on a flat green. On `GridHeightEvidence.green_contour()`, a 9 yd/s uphill putt from the front tier ends within 1.5 yd of its flat-green distance, a 3 yd/s sidehill putt across a step breaks between 0.1 and 1 yd, a ball set down still on a step's slope rolls down to the lower shelf, and an uphill putt that stops short on a step rolls back. Shots on other surfaces are unchanged and the non-green ball tests pass as they are. The green-slope tests (`test_ball_at_rest_on_a_green_slope_rolls_down`, `test_ball_rolling_downhill_on_green_speeds_up`, `test_uphill_putt_that_stops_short_rolls_back`) keep their assertions but may change their ramp or speeds, and `test_slope_pull_beats_rest_friction` compares the scaled pull with each surface's constant friction. The `docs/design.md` Ball lines on friction, slope pull and rest speed are updated in this story, and it gets a `/balance surfaces` target. `docs/decisions/e1-s9-probe.txt` gains still-ball and slow-ball cases on the scaled green.
  - Test: cases in `tests/test_ball.gd`.
  - Touches: `golf/`, `course/`, `tests/`
- [x] E1-S12 Settle green balls in seconds, not half a minute. A putt should finish while the player still cares.
  - Note: found in E1-S11 review. Linear drag gives a slow ball on a green slope a terminal speed, about 0.16 yd/s with the E1-S11 values and only about 0.5 yd/s even if the constant friction never ramped back up (pull 0.32 less the 0.08 still-ball friction, over the green drag of 0.47 per second). So a ball set down on a contour step takes up to 28 s to reach the shelf, uphill putts that roll back take 12 to 35 s, a 3 yd/s sidehill putt runs 2.5 yd forward and then slides 2.5 yd sideways for 13 s, and on a green that rises one step per tile for several tiles a still ball hits the 60 s cap mid-slope. A sweep of `rest_decel_scale`, `decel_ramp_speed` and `drag_scale` found no setting that meets the E1-S11 putt targets and brings a multi-tile green ramp under the cap. Could move to E2, before E2-S7, at `/next-epic`.
  - Needs: a user answer, recorded with `/decision`. Is the sidehill "break" read where the putt runs out of pace (as E1-S11 measures it, 0.38 yd) or where the ball comes to rest (2.5 yd today)? And must a still ball on any green slope roll to the shelf, or may a ball rest on a felt slope below some threshold, as a real green holds a ball on 3%? Allowing rest changes the E1-S11 still-ball clause and makes most of this story a retune.
  - Accept: a `/balance surfaces` target in `docs/design.md` for the longest green settle time (for example 8 s), measured on `GridHeightEvidence.green_contour()` and on a green rising one step per tile from height 0 to 7. Every still ball and every putt from 1 to 15 yd/s on both settles under it, with steps below `max_steps`. The E1-S11 putt targets, or a widened band agreed through `/balance`, still hold.
  - Test: cases in `tests/test_ball.gd`, including a still ball on a several-tile one-step-per-tile green ramp that reaches flat ground with steps < `max_steps`, and `test_ball_at_rest_on_a_green_slope_rolls_down` back at its original x 20 start.
  - Done (D7): a green holds a slow ball on a felt slope up to about 4.5% (`rest_decel_scale` 0.45, `decel_ramp_speed` 1.5) and the break is read at rest (0.34 yd). Longest settle for a ball resting on green 5.5 s on the contour (axes and diagonals), 5.6 s on the ramp; a putt that runs off onto the rough's slope can take about 9.6 s (rough physics, known limit). On the one-step ramp the still ball and the x 20 start are held where set, and the tests say so; the roll-down (still from x 20) and roll-back tests moved to a two-steps-per-tile green. Open, before E2-S7 (D7 Inconclusive): greens steeper than the hold still trickle (still balls reach the cap mid-slope, roll-backs 8 to 28 s), and resting balls are drawn off the flat tile tops.
  - Touches: `golf/`, `course/`, `tests/`, `docs/`
- [x] E1-S13 Rest a ball that settles in a dip. A ball in a one-tile pit should stop, not rock for 60 s.
  - Note: found in E1-S11 review, and on main before it, on every rolling surface. A still ball in a one-tile pit hits the 3600-tick cap on green and on fairway: the static branch of `Ball._step_roll` adds the pull less the friction and nothing damps the swing across the gradient jump at the tile centre.
  - Accept: in the static branch, a ball whose pull reverses against its velocity (it is at a low point) rests, or the hold is applied against the velocity before the pull is added. Other ball tests pass as they are.
  - Test: a case in `tests/test_ball.gd`: a still ball set just off the centre of a one-tile pit, on green and on fairway, rests within 1 s (60 ticks) near the centre.
  - Touches: `golf/`, `tests/`

## E2: One hole — done

Ships as 0.2.0. Serves pillars 1 and 2: one click from the tee, honest shots.

Done when: you can design a single hole, stand on its tee in one click, play it to the cup, and read your score.

Planning notes:

- The user answered the four planning forks on 2026-11-03: cut wind, paint `oob` from the Build palette, fix steep greens in the physics (E2-S4), and snap the drawn rest height to the tile top (E2-S7). E2-S1 records these answers with `/decision`; it does not reopen them.
- The E1 review follow-ups (old E2-S10 to E2-S20) are folded in, and none is dropped. The settle cases and steep greens go into E2-S4. The resting-ball drawing goes into E2-S7. The D7 mirror and the E1-S12 commit note go into E2-S1. The hand checks go into E2-S9.
- Two-folder rule: some paths are listed in Touches but do not count toward a story's two folders. They are `tests/` (including `tests/fixtures/`), the smoke harness (`scripts/playtest.gd` and its fixture `data/playtest_smoke.json`), and edits to `docs/design.md` lines or `docs/decisions/` evidence that record the story's own change. They count as test and doc upkeep, not scope.
- E2-S1 settles the rules and targets that cross stories or need the user. A story that only sets its own starting numbers against those targets lists them under Settles and writes them into `docs/design.md` in the same commit, as E1-S6 did.
- New markers (tee, pin, aim line, landing marker, swing bar) are drawn in code with colours from `art/palette.gpl`. Each has a shape as well as a colour, like the E1 ball and landing marker. Their art specs come with the final art (E5-S1, E5-S3).

The Claude Docs design doc mirror (D7 and D8 to D18, wind removed from Aim and the Play toolbar) is applied by the orchestrator, not from this branch. One departure from a recommendation: lie slope is read from the heights around the ball (rise 5 yd ahead, tilt across a 10 yd window), not from the gradient at the rest point (D14 says why).
  - Note: why a spike comes first. The milestone's only design-doc open question, "Swing bar feel", closes in E2-S9. Every other story depends on choices that need the user or a `/decision`: wind, out of bounds and the plot, where the Claude Docs design doc and `docs/design.md` disagree, plus steep greens and the drawn rest height, left open by D7. They also depend on rules that cross stories: the shot model is shared by E2-S5 and E2-S7, and the hazard and cup rules by E2-S6 and E2-S8. Measured starting values stay with the story that tunes them.
  - Accept: each item under Settles has a line in `docs/design.md`, and a `/decision` where it is a real choice. The planning forks are recorded as the user answered them, not reopened. Wind and OB each get a `/decision`. The Claude Docs design doc gets D7 (a green holds a ball on a gentle slope, and the break is read at rest) and every decision made here. If wind is cut, that edit removes it from the Play toolbar and the aim marker. If the user declines a design doc edit, the decision says so. DECISIONS.md is append-only, so D7 is not edited. Instead, the `/decision` that sets the settle targets ends with this line: "The E1-S12 commit body describes the rest rule from before D7's per-axis wording; `docs/design.md` Ball is current." A hand calculation in `docs/design.md` shows that the chosen bar and lie rules can reach the bunker target (SW at 50% lands 20 to 40 yd) with a plausible SW carry, before E2-S5 tunes it.
  - Test: none. Review the `docs/design.md` and DECISIONS.md diffs, including that the settle decision carries the E1-S12 line, and the Claude Docs edit.
  - Touches: `docs/`
  - Settles:
    - Wind: cut (user, 2026-11-03). The `/decision` says what this removes from the design doc; the Icebox "Weather" entry already carries the wind note.
    - Out of bounds: off the map plus `oob` tiles, painted from the Build palette (E2-S3) and read from files (user, 2026-11-03). Placed OB stakes stay in the Icebox.
    - Plot: its size, and the surface and height of a blank plot. It must fit a par 5 (more than 94 tiles long) with water on its line for E2-S9, and 9 holes for E3-S2. Its cost is left to E4-S1.
    - Hole rules. Rule 3's path: 4 or 8 neighbours, and which surfaces it may cross (recommended: 4 neighbours, every surface except water and `oob`, trees included, heights ignored). Rule 4's yardage measure (recommended: a straight line from tee centre to cup centre at 5 yd per tile) and its rounding at exactly 250 and 470 yd. Rule 5's landing zone: its shape and size from each tee. A hole's footprint: which tiles count as being under a hole, so that an edit there closes it.
    - Stroke limit: the most strokes on a hole before the ball is picked up, and what the score records. E3-S3 guests use the same limit.
    - Club legality. Where the driver is allowed (recommended: on a hole's first stroke from its tee marker, and on any tee tile, so a tee marker on fairway still allows it). Whether the putter is green only. The sand wedge is bunker only.
    - Shot model:
      - whether bar percentage maps to launch speed or to carry;
      - whether lie multipliers scale carry (recommended, to match the Distance column) or launch speed;
      - how a club's roll factor combines with the surface's Roll and bounce;
      - what hook, slice and sidehill curve are. Recommended: a heading offset at launch, with no new Ball term. A lateral acceleration in flight would be a new `golf/ball_params.tres` term with its own `/decision`;
      - how the lie slope is measured: the gradient at the rest point, projected along the aim for uphill and across it for sidehill, in steps per tile;
      - whether rough's 0.8 to 0.9 is fixed or rolled. If rolled, it comes from the ball's own seed, so the prediction matches the flight.
    - Aim marker: for a full club it shows where a perfect swing lands (100% power, accuracy at centre). For the putter it shows where the putt comes to rest. A perfect swing lands within 1 tile of the marker (E1-S7).
    - Cup and hazards:
      - the cup sits at its tile centre and captures on any surface, checked on ground ticks only, or also at touchdown;
      - the water and OB drop point: the exact "last entry point", and how far back onto playable ground the ball is dropped;
      - what a ball that rolls into a tree tile does;
      - how the 1 to 2 tile tree scatter picks a target that is not trees, water or OB.
    - Settle targets: the longest settle off the green on every rolling surface the builder can paint, on greens steeper than one step per tile (fixed in the physics by E2-S4, user 2026-11-03), and for a putt that runs off the green. The 9.6 s off-green diagonal putt either meets its target or is recorded as an accepted limit, with the reason.
    - Drawn rest height: snap the drawn ball and shadow to the top of the tile under them while on the ground (user, 2026-11-03; built in E2-S7).
    - `/balance` targets: copy all five target lines from `.claude/skills/balance/SKILL.md` into `docs/design.md`, which the skill credits. Restate three of them, and note the restatement in the decision:
      - "putter never leaves the green" becomes "the putter is selectable only on green, and a full putt rolls at most N yd on a flat green". E2-S5 sets N with the putter speed range;
      - "at 100% for a skill-10 golfer" becomes "a perfect swing, until guests have skill in E3";
      - "about one club" becomes "within one club step (10 to 12 yd)", and the sidehill curve target names its club.
      The reactions and economy lines are copied unchanged for E3 and E4.

- [x] E2-S4 Settle every ball the builder can make. No ball in play may roll for half a minute or reach the 60 s cap. This lands before E2-S7, because live play makes the player wait out every settle in real time.
  - Done: four changes in `golf/` and `course/surfaces.tres`, all in `docs/design.md` Ball. The gradient at an exact tile centre is the mean of both sides. A concave crease (where the ground turns up against the ball at a tile centre) keeps, per axis, only the velocity along the new ground, which damps valley, pit and gully swings; it never turns the ball back, a lip costs nothing, and at the foot of a steep green bank it feels only `slope_scale`, so a putt still climbs a 2- to 7-step tier (12.8 to 14.2 yd/s from 7.5 yd short). A low point is checked per axis speed, and a stopped axis sits on the low point's line. Rough and trees fade their drag below 12 yd/s (`drag_full_speed`), so slow balls run down rough slopes instead of crawling. Steep greens are fixed in the physics, as the user chose, but by the felt slope, not friction: above `steep_from` 0.6 on each axis a green feels `steep_scale` 1.5 of the extra slope, so one-step contours are unchanged. Friction alone cannot do it (the 6% felt pull is 0.64 yd/s², 6.7 s from the top of a two-steps-per-tile bank with no friction at all). Cart path `drag_scale` 1.1 brings drives down a cart path stepping 7 to 0 (every 1 to 4 tiles, 100% and 110%) under 10 s now that a lip no longer brakes the ball. Every case below meets its target, plus one-tile pits and trenches 2 to 7 steps deep on every rolling surface. The tightest is the stepped cart-path drive at 9.9 s (110%, every 4 tiles). The debug shot still lands on (7, 4) and rests on (12, 4), at x 64.7 instead of 63.1. Known limits in `docs/design.md` Ball, for `/balance surfaces` before E2-S7: fast downhill putts over a steep green bank (up to 12.7 s at 14 yd/s on two steps per tile, over the E2-S5 putter range), V valleys 5 to 7 steps deep (up to 13.5 s on rough), and still balls set on the rim of a two-step green pit, which creep at the rest speed (up to 20.7 s).

S15 and S18 come in too: on greens steeper than the D7 hold, still balls reach the cap mid-slope and roll-backs take 8 to 28 s. So does the guard for S20, a diagonal putt off the contour that takes about 9.6 s on rough. Order of work:

## E6: Land — done

Ships as 0.3.0. Runs before E3 (D19). Serves pillars 3 and 1: the course starts as land the player buys and shapes, and a hole can only go on land they own, so it is still playable the moment it is placed.

Done when: a new course opens on generated natural land, the player owns a starting block, buys neighbouring parcels from a land budget, and builds and plays a hole on them. New sandbox still gives free, fully owned land.

- [x] E6-S1 Settle the land rules on paper. D19 chose the model and left the numbers open.
  - Accept: each item below has a line in `docs/design.md`, and a `/decision` where it is a real choice.
  - Test: none. Review the design.md diff.
  - Touches: `docs/`
  - User answered 2026-11-03: 16 x 16 parcels, a 2 x 2 starting block, and a ball at rest on unowned land is out of bounds. Record them with `/decision`; do not reopen them.
  - Settles:
    - Parcel size. Proposed: 16 x 16 tiles (80 yd), so the 128 x 128 map is 8 x 8 parcels and D10's draw and frame times are unchanged.
    - The starting block. Proposed: 2 x 2 parcels near the middle of the map, 160 yd on a side, room for a par 3 but not a par 4, so the first purchase comes with the second hole.
    - Which parcels may be bought. Proposed: any parcel sharing an edge with one already owned.
    - Parcel price: a base price, plus or minus what is on it (trees, water, hills). Prices live in `data/`.
    - The land budget until E4. Proposed: $5,000, spent on parcels only. E4-S1 folds it into the one balance.
    - What a ball does on land the player does not own. Proposed: it plays as OB (D9), drawn as a boundary, so buying land is how a hole gets room.
    - Whether land can be sold back. Proposed: no.
    - What the generator lays down: hill heights within 0 to 7, tree stands, ponds, rough, and how often; and that it is seeded, so one seed gives one map.
  - Done: D26 (16 x 16 parcels, a free 2 x 2 starting block at the centre, ownership in CourseData, format v2), D27 (buy across an edge only, no selling back, undo refunds), D28 (land budget $5,000; provisional price $1,000 + $100 per step of relief - $1 per tree tile - $2 per water tile, $500 to $2,000, in `data/land.json`), D29 (unowned land is OB as an `oob` tile is), D30 (a hole owns its tee, its cup and a rule-3 path, not its whole footprint; new rule 6). `docs/design.md` Land holds every number, the generator's rates and the hand calculation: the block fits a 160 yd par 3 and no par 4 (219 yd at most), one purchase fits a par 4 (281 yd). E6-S4 and E6-S7's Accept lines now follow D29 and D30.
- [x] E6-S2 Generate natural land. The map stops being blank.
  - Accept: a seeded generator in `course/` fills the map with hills, tree stands, ponds and rough, using only the existing surfaces and heights 0 to 7, at the rates E6-S1 set. The same seed gives the same map. Every parcel has some buildable ground. Tunables live in a resource.
  - Test: `tests/test_land_generator.gd`: same seed same map, heights in range, rates within tolerance, no parcel all water.
  - Touches: `course/`, `tests/`
  - Done: `LandGenerator.generate(seed)` (`course/land_generator.gd`, about 45 ms on 128 x 128) with `course/land_tuning.tres`. Hills are cosine bells on 2 x 2 tile terrace cells clamped to one step between cells, so the slope rule (one step, one riser every 2 tiles) holds exactly; one radius factor is bisected so 15.0% of the map is at height 4 or more. Ponds grow by 2 x 2 squares on ground flat for 2 tiles round and are dug one step; stands grow tile by tile and never touch. On seeds 1 to 20 trees are 11.0% to 12.1% and water 3.8% to 4.1%; every parcel is at least 60% rough and the starting block at least 85% rough and dry (every check also held over seeds 1 to 150). `LandGenerator.start_block` gives D26's block; the generator keeps its own `PARCEL_TILES` 16 until E6-S3's `CourseData.PARCEL_TILES` lands. `generate hilly` keeps its ridge for now. Screenshot fixture `tests/fixtures/land_shot.tscn`.

- [x] E6-S12 Keep the putter's marker right on steep greens and inside a frame. PuttAim misses the nearest putt on steep banks and can drop frames.
  - Note: moved from E6-S12 when E2 closed (user, 2026-11-03), so 0.2.0 could ship.
  - Note: (found in E2 review) from E2-S20. When PuttAim pins at the bar's edge, or the secant slope is not positive, it stops: on banks of two steps per tile the marker can sit up to about 4 yd off the nearest putt. One Ball tick costs about 36 µs; a cold solve is about 22 ms on gentle greens and 173 ms on a steep one, and sweeping the aim over the GridHeightEvidence contour reaches 21.5 ms, over a 16.7 ms frame. E3 guests that call PuttAim (10 to 36 ms each on gentle greens) will need the same budget.
  - Accept: on a pin or a non-positive secant, PuttAim runs a coarse bracket scan (about 5 powers) before it stops, so on two-steps-per-tile banks the marker rests within 1 yd of the brute-force nearest putt. A solve is capped by ticks or time per frame, or spread over frames (a first answer, then refined), so no aim update takes more than one frame; the cap lives in a `.tres`. `docs/design.md` Aim marker states the cost and the cap.
  - Test: steep-bank cases in `tests/test_shot_prediction.gd` against the brute-force check; a budget case in `tests/test_swing.gd`.
  - Touches: `golf/`, `scenes/`
  - Done: `PuttAim` (`golf/putt_aim.gd`) runs its secant on the roll's reach along the aim (how far it got before it stopped or turned back), which keeps growing with power on a bank a putt rolls back down; a roll that turns back level with the cup without dropping is aimed again to turn a `cup_radius` past it, so it drops on the way back (it finds capture windows 1 to 2% of the bar wide). On a pin, a non-positive secant, a false-position bracket narrower than `putt_aim_refine_power` (a jump in the reach where a putt crests a riser) or a second turn-back it scans `putt_aim_scan_powers` 5 powers, bisects for the roll-back edge (the hardest putt that stops short without rolling back, when the scan shows one) and narrows a golden-section search on the distance to the cup around the nearest, to `putt_aim_refine_power` 0.02; `putt_aim_max_runs` rose from 8 to 16. On banks two steps per tile, 210 cold solves (90 on a bank rising along x, 48 along y, 72 on a diagonal staircase; uphill, downhill, aims up to 8 degrees off) are all within 1 yd of a 1%-step brute force (worst 0.56 yd; before E6-S12, 45 of 60 uphill solves were more than 0.5 yd off, worst 7.3 yd; the review found a diagonal case 2.75 yd off before the edge search and one 1.7 yd off before the jump rule); `tests/test_shot_prediction.gd` checks nine of them against it. The solve is resumable (`PuttAim.begin`, `advance(max_ticks, max_usec)`, pausing inside a roll through `ShotPrediction.launched` and `of_ball`), and SwingControl's `_process` steps it once a frame, at most `putt_aim_frame_ticks` 160 Ball ticks and `putt_aim_frame_usec` 6,000 µs (`golf/swing_tuning.tres`), showing the best putt so far; starting a solve (a turn, a club change, a ball at rest) steps none of it, so the frame a ball rests spends one budget. A turned aim waits for the running solve's first putt to rest, then starts warm from it, so a sweep never throws every putt away mid-roll. `settle_aim` finishes a solve at once for tests and fixtures. Measured (`tests/test_swing.gd`): a cold steep solve of 3,280 ticks spreads over 21 frames, worst update 6.4 ms, mean 6.2 ms; sweeping 1 degree a frame on the steep downhill banks, the marker moves every 3 to 5 frames and its putt trails the aim by at most 8 degrees (before the review: frozen for the whole sweep, up to 30 degrees behind). `PuttAim.solve` still runs a whole solve at once, with the same result as any budget. Screenshot `docs/decisions/e6-s12-putt-marker-steep.png` (`tests/fixtures/putt_marker_steep_shot.tscn`). `docs/design.md` Aim marker states the accuracy, cost and cap. No decision was needed (D31 unused).

## E3: Nine holes and guests — done

Ships as 0.4.0 (D19). Serves pillar 3: the course tells a story through its guests. No money yet, so arrivals are a flat number until E4 connects rating.

Done when: nine holes are open, guests play them in order, and their complaints name a hole you can click and fix. Nine holes are checked in New sandbox, where every parcel is owned (user, 2026-11-05, at `/next-epic`): a New course's $5,000 land budget buys about 4 parcels and nine holes need about 34, so on a New course guests play the one or two holes it can afford, and E4's money buys the rest. Guests play whatever holes are open, on any course.

Planning notes (E2-S19, from the E2 review; `/next-epic` read these on 2026-11-05 and folded them into the stories below; they stay as background):

- E6 Land runs before E3 (D19). By the time E3 starts, a course opens on generated land and holes go only on owned parcels; E3's stories assume that land, not the blank D10 plot, except in New sandbox.
- Hole picking: the tee and cup tools build hole 1 only (`BuildTool.hole_number` is fixed at 1, `scenes/build_tool.gd`). E3-S2 needs a way to pick which hole the tools build.
- Landing zones: D11 checks one landing zone per hole, from the tee. A par 5's second-shot zone is not checked, and doglegs are measured on the straight tee-to-cup line. Revisit if E3 guests hit each other on par 5s (with E3-S1's line-of-play item).
- Validator cost: it reruns after every click edit, about 8.5 ms on 128 x 128 (mostly the surface grid and the rule 3 component graph), and once at a drag's end. With nine holes, cache the graph between edits, or move it to native code, if frames hitch (E3-S2's 60 fps check).
- Guest club choice: the riser-foot rule (D20, E2-S10) covers it. Guests choose clubs with `ClubSet.is_blocked` and skip a blocked club; a blocked club stays legal for the player. E3-S3 reuses it.
- Trees: a flat surface pattern, with nothing drawn for their 10 yd (`block_height`) height, so the block cannot be seen. A note for E5-S3's tree art and for `/balance`; guests hitting into trees in E3 will make it visible.
- Camera framing: Main frames the camera at the course centre at 1x. When a course with holes is loaded, framing the first hole may read better (E3-S2's hole list, or E6-S6's start on land).
- E3's stories predate D19 and E6 (E2-S19 review). The `/next-epic` run that activates E3 checks them against land ownership, for example E3-S2's 9-hole fps check on the generated map, and guests walking over unowned land.
- Dense issue clusters (E2-S17): with 25 or more faults close together, sideways boxes run off-screen at 1x and their leaders become long diagonals, and a collapsed box shows only its badge, so its reasons are read only in the hole panel. If nine-hole playtests find that hard to read, show text only for the hovered or selected hole, or add a '+N more' count.
- Guest putting (E2-S20, E6-S12): a PuttAim solve costs about 10 to 36 ms on gentle greens and, since E6-S12 (up to 16 putts with the scan, the roll-back edge and the golden search), about 200 to 300 ms at once on a green rising two steps per tile. A guest must not call `PuttAim.solve` on a steep green in one go: run it through `PuttAim.begin` and `advance(max_ticks, max_usec)` with a tick or time budget per simulated second (as SwingControl does per frame), or with a lower `putt_aim_max_runs`. E3-S3 and E3-S4 inherit this. Green design: on the GridHeightEvidence contour a putt from the bottom terrace cannot reach a cup two terraces up and about 29 yd away (a full putt rolls 27.5 yd on flat); the marker then shows the 100% putt.
- Routing on land (E6 review): the D28 budget buys 4 to 5 parcels and nine holes need about 34 (E6-S8), and D30 lets a hole run narrow beside the OB line; on the E6-S8 lanes a one-parcel (80 yd) par 4 corridor sent 29% of swept tee strokes within 60 degrees of the line OB. E3-S2 and E3-S3 should check how guests route and score on such holes.

- [x] E3-S1 Settle the numbers guests need. `docs/design.md` names the checks but not what they measure.
  - Done 2026-11-05 (D38 to D43, the user's answers to the spike's measured options; the spike's settled items without a decision): `docs/design.md` Guests and reactions has a line for every item, and `data/guests.json` holds every guest tunable with its starting value (no `guests/` code yet). Round length (D38): about one day a nine-hole round, about 20 s a hole, walk 40 yd/s, 1 s a stroke plus green reading, on-screen guest flights drawn 5x faster, guests at dusk react to the holes they finished and leave. Reaction (D39): one per hole played, praise (pass 4), complaint (fail 2 or more, a doubled check counting two) or a quiet neutral, each naming its hole; the rating averages the last 50. Skill (D40): click sigma 5% (skill 10) to 13% (skill 1), putts 3% to 7% aim and 4.5% to 12% power, expected score par + 2 × (10 - skill) / 9, challenge within 2. Aim (D41): at the cup, club by distance, checked with `ShotPrediction`, skipping blocked or water and OB plans, then aims of +8, -8, +16, -16 degrees, then clubbing down. Tastes (D42): Purist (fair double), Adventurer (interesting double), Casual (challenge within 3). Blind (D43): a 2 yd mark at the target hidden from an eye 1.75 yd above the tee, sampled every 1 yd over drawn tops and 10 yd trees, target the zone centre from 300 yd, else the cup. Settled from the spike: 16 guests a day in groups of 1 to 4 over the first half of the day from the first open hole's tee; guests walk over unowned land while their ball obeys D29; a tick budget (480 a guest, 4,800 the sim, per simulated second, carried over as "reading the green") and 6 putt solve runs for guests; pace by wait (6 to 24 s) and walk (70 to 160 yd) limits from patience 1 to 10; interesting as a hazard on rule 3's line plus a safe 4-neighbour route, static per hole; rule 4 stays the straight line, for E3-S8 to recheck on doglegs.

  - Settles:
    - Round length. A day is 180 simulated seconds, so a 9-hole round must fit in about 20 seconds per hole or span days. This sets walk speed and shot time.
    - Arrivals until E4: a flat number of guests per day, and group size.
    - Skill to noise on power and accuracy, and expected score per skill for the challenge check.
    - How a guest picks a club and an aim on a hole with no landing zone checked past the tee (D11 checks one, from the tee; a par 5's second shot and a dogleg's corner are not), and on a narrow land corridor (D30; on the E6-S8 lanes a one-parcel par 4 sent 29% of swept tee strokes within 60 degrees of the line OB).
    - Guests and unowned land: a guest's ball obeys D29 like the player's; may a guest walk over unowned land to reach it, or round it? Proposed: walk over it (it is out of bounds for the ball, not a wall).
    - Guest putting cost: a tick or time budget per guest per simulated second for `PuttAim.begin` / `advance` (planning notes, Guest putting), and a lower `putt_aim_max_runs` for guests if that is the cheaper choice.
    - Patience scale and how it fails the pace check: wait time and walk distance limits.
    - Taste list and what each taste changes.
    - How "interesting" is detected. Proposed: a hazard on the direct line plus a safe route that is longer.
    - What "could not see from the tee" means for the fair check: line of sight over the height map.
    - What one reaction is. The design doc has a reaction per guest at the last hole and also per hole; rating averages the last 50, so the unit matters.
    - Revisit rule 4 yardage along the line of play. E2-S1 measured a straight line from tee to cup; doglegs on a nine-hole course may want the line of play.

A slot whose tee, cup or par changed, or with a written tile in its D11 footprint (`CourseValidator.in_footprint`, the same tiles whose edit closes an open hole), has its reactions so far marked `ReactionFeed.CLEARED`: they keep their place in the 50-reaction window but count for no hole in `slot_tallies`, `tally` or `tallies`, and undoing the edit does not bring them back. A renumber, a swap, Open and Close keep the tally.   - Touches: `scenes/`, `tests/`
  - Done 2026-11-07: `CourseView.layout_issue_labels` treats every tee's hole number (`HoleMarker.label_rect`) and every issue badge (`IssueMarker.badge_rect`) as labels a box must not cover, and the framed hole's line of play (E3-S46; `CourseView.framed_slot`, set by `Main.frame_hole`, cut into half-tile screen rects, `framed_line_rects`) as one it keeps off where it can: a first try with the line, with sideways tries just past the line's pieces as well as past the boxes, then without it. A box with no room collapses to its badge as before, so crowded boxes collapse at the farther steps. Sideways tries start only past boxes and the line, not past each label (with 196 faults that kept the layout under its 100 ms bound). The badge now keeps its screen size (14 px, `badge_half` 7) on the tile's right corner and, on a tee, stands `BADGE_GAP` 2 px past the tee's number (`badge_clear`), so it never covers "4a". A tee's number is drawn by a child of its marker (`HoleMarker.label`) that rises over the terrain (z 1) only where drawn ground on a row in front covers its bottom edge (`label_covered`: a ray cast at its bottom corners and middle, only when a tile within 4 in front stands higher), so on flat ground it still sorts with the terrain and the ball on the tee draws over it; it is checked again on each zoom change and height edit. On nine_holes with every hole failing, the boxes did not need to collapse to fit at 0.175x (9 boxes on a 1434 px map); the dense-cluster test covers collapsing. Tests (`tests/test_issue_marker.gd`, 4 new): no two labels (boxes, tee numbers, badges) overlap on nine_holes with every hole failing at 0.175x, 0.5x and 1x, each box within its stack and side limits; a rule-1 badge on a shared number's tee clears its "2a"/"2b" at 0.175x to 2x; a tee behind a knoll five steps up has its number lifted at 0.5x, on flat ground not; a framed hole's box over its line moves off it and still shows. Screenshots looked at: `docs/decisions/e3-s40-every-hole-0.175x.png` (every hole failing, holes 4 and 5 sharing number 4: no box over another, a tee number or a badge; "4a" and "4b" with their badges beside them; three boxes collapsed), `e3-s40-tee-behind-bank-0.5x.png` (hole 1's tee hidden by a knoll in front, its "1" drawn over the knoll), `e3-s40-framed-hole-3.png` (hole 3 framed with every hole failing). - [x] E3-S51 Make putts that roll at the cup drop at a fair rate. A putt struck at the right pace drops more often than it rolls over.
  - Note: (found in the E3-S30 hand playtest) the user's putts roll over or past the cup. `cup_radius` 0.25 yd and `cup_capture_speed` 2.0 yd/s capture a putt finishing up to about 1.3 yd past on a flat green, but the bar maps 1 to 18 yd/s across it, so 1% of the bar is 0.17 yd/s and a 5 yd putt's pace window is a few percent of the bar.
  - Accept: a putting target is added to docs/design.md Balance targets (a human-like click error, `power_sigma` and `accuracy_sigma` set from a skill-7 guest's D40 putt sigmas): made from 1 yd at least 90%, 3 yd 55 to 75%, 8 yd 15 to 30%, on a flat green and on a one-step contour, with three-putts from 8 yd under 15%; `/balance putting` (a new `--table putting` in `scripts/sim.gd`) measures it, and the tune (capture speed, capture radius, the putter's speed range or a distance-scaled bar, whichever moves least) meets it. Any change to putting numbers reruns `/balance reactions` and E3-S24's scoring edges and keeps them in target.
  - Test: `tests/test_balance_putting.gd` asserts the target at `--runs 200`; `tests/test_ball.gd` keeps the capture rule's cases.
  - Touches: `golf/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`
  - Done 2026-11-06: D49. `--table putting` (`scripts/putting_sim.gd`, `PuttingSim`): a skill-7 click error (power 7%, aim 4.3%) on the marker's pace, reading the break. Before: flat 47 / 39 / 30% made from 1 / 3 / 8 yd, 37% three-putts from 8; contour 50 / 31 / 23%, 42%. Tuned: `putt_bar_curve` 0.9 (a near-geometric bar, `Club.putt_speed_at`), a centred pace in `PuttAim` (`putt_aim_past_yd` 0.5, `putt_aim_past_tolerance_yd` 0.15: once a putt holes, dry putts aim the roll 0.5 yd past and keep that pace if it drops), `cup_radius` 0.3; `cup_capture_speed` stays 2.0. After: flat 92 / 69 / 32%, 16%; contour 95 / 65 / 27%, 18%. Target refined (design.md Balance targets, putting): 8 yd 15 to 35% (was 30) and three-putts at most 20% (was 15), since the flat green runs 6 to 12 points over the contour at every tune. Re-pinned the steep-bank nearest putts (`tests/test_shot_prediction.gd`, from `--full`) and the noisy-chip fallbacks (`tests/test_round_flow.gd`, one left: stroke 5 at 105 yd); fixed `tests/test_guest_brain.gd`'s crossing check for a group that finished its last hole the same second. `/balance reactions` (seed 1, 50): skill 10 on the spike holes -0.11 (was +0.11), fair par 4 praise 74% (64%), complaints 0%; but two pace-across-shapes rows now miss (E3-S54).

- [x] E3-S54 Retune pace across hole shapes after E3-S51's putting. The fair par 4 plays faster, so the gap rows miss.
  - Note: (found in E3-S51) better putting (D49) cut the fair par 4's pace fails from 26% to 21% at seed 1 (20% to 15% at seed 2) and lifted its praise to 74% (92% at seed 2), while the nine-hole par 5 (hole 3, 36%) and the dogleg (33%) did not move, since their waits are tee bunching and pond carries, not time on the green. So `pace fails: par 5 less the fair par 4's` is +0.14 and the dogleg's +0.12 against the 10-point band. Before E3-S51, seed 2 already missed the par 5 row (+0.16) and praise (76%), so the band sits on the edge of the field's noise. Tried in E3-S51 at seed 1, none in target: `wait_base_s` 5 (+0.17, +0.15), `play_clear_share` 0.2 (+0.22, +0.11) and 0.15 (+0.29, +0.17), `play_clear_yd` 45 (+0.11, +0.12).
  - Accept: `/balance reactions --runs 50 --seed 1` hits every target again by numbers in `data/guests.json`, or a `/decision` restates the pace-across-shapes target (for example read over seeds 1 to 3); seeds 2 and 3 are recorded.
  - Test: `tests/test_balance_reactions.gd` keeps passing.
  - Touches: `data/`, `docs/`
  - Done 2026-11-07: `/balance reactions --runs 50 --seed 1` hits every target by numbers in `data/guests.json`: `play_clear_yd` 25 (50), `cross_clear_yd` 15 (20), `wait_base_s` 8 (6), `wait_per_patience_s` 0.8 (1), so pace fails past 8.8 to 16 s of waiting. Measured on main with E3-S38 and E3-S39 (147bcdf), seed 1 before / after: fair par 4 praise 74 / 74%, complaints 0 / 0%, pace fails 21 / 21%; first tee +8.7 / +6.8 points, par 5 +11.8 / -4.5, dogleg +12.0 / +8.0, hole 8 (recorded) +17.4 / +17.4; blind 0% praise, 52% complaints; crossing holes 6 / 0% of records held; scoring rows unchanged. Seed 2: praise 92 / 92% (misses, as before), first tee +14.7 / +12.8 (misses both), par 5 +17.8 / +1.5, dogleg +11.3 / +9.3. Seed 3: praise 90 / 90% (misses), first tee +12.0 / +10.8 (misses both), par 5 +15.1 / -0.5, dogleg +6.0 / +4.0. So the par 5 and dogleg rows hold on seeds 1 to 3; the first tee, which E3-S38 and E3-S39 raised to 28 to 30% (21% before them), and the fair par 4's praise band hold on seed 1 only, and no pace number moved seeds 2 and 3's praise off 90 and 92% (swept before E3-S38 and E3-S39 landed: `play_clear_yd` 25 to 40, `cross_clear_yd` 10 to 20, `wait_base_s` 4 to 9 with 0.7 to 1.5 a patience point, `play_clear_share` 0.2, `flight_draw_speed` 10, `arrival_window_s` 120; docs/design.md Balance targets, reactions). `tests/test_guest_brain.gd`'s crossing hold check reads day 27 for 18 (at `cross_clear_yd` 15 no hole 1 guest is held on days 1, 2 or 18).

- [x] E3-S56 Draw the green view as a topographic map. Smooth contour lines traced from the ground the ball rolls on replace the stepped edges and the arrow on every tile.
  - Note: (user, E3-S30 hand playtest, 2026-11-06) "i want topo maps on the whole course, and the greens". The ball rolls on `HeightField.height_at`, bilinear between tile centres, but E3-S52's panel draws contours on stepped tile edges and an arrow on every tile, so it shows a staircase the ball never feels. Answers the playtest's arrows question (24).
  - Accept: a shared tracer (`golf/`, reads HeightField only) returns contour polylines at a given level over a tile window (marching squares on the tile-centre grid, which is exact for the bilinear field). The green view draws: a line every half step (interval in the `.tres`), a heavier index line every whole step, short downhill ticks on the low side of each line, the height shade smoothed (per pixel or sub-tile, not per tile), and fall arrows only on tiles whose fall reaches a steep threshold (2 steps a tile, in the `.tres`). Flat greens show no lines. Aim, dots, marker and click-to-swing are unchanged. Readable in grayscale.
  - Test: `tests/test_contours.gd`: on a known ramp the level-h line lies where `height_at` equals h (sampled within a tolerance), a flat window yields none, a closed hill yields a closed loop, ticks point downhill. `tests/test_green_view.gd` keeps passing, steep-only arrows asserted. Screenshots on the flat, contoured and tiered fixtures and a playtest-nine green, plus grayscale (`docs/decisions/e3-s56-*.png`), looked at.
  - Touches: `golf/`, `scenes/`, `tests/`
  - Done: `golf/contour_tracer.gd` (`ContourTracer`, reads HeightField only): marching squares on the tile-centre grid plus the map edge where the grid meets it (the edge height holds there), each cell cut `subdiv` a side (sub-cells are exactly bilinear, saddles decided by the centre mean), crossings by linear interpolation along cell edges (exact), segments oriented uphill-on-the-left and chained by edge id, clipped to the window's tiles (Liang-Barsky), specks under 0.05 yd dropped; `trace_levels` does every level in one pass over the non-flat cells, `levels_between`, `height_range` and `ticks` (downhill, on the right). Integer heights make a whole-step level degenerate (a terrace sits exactly on it), so a level is traced a hair (1e-4 steps) below itself: its line hugs the terrace's falling edge through the edge tiles' centres, never both sides of a flat terrace, and flat ground has none. The green view: tiles at full colour, then the height shade as an ink triangle mesh with per-vertex alpha (1 - shade of `height_at`, `shade_subdiv` 4 a tile), lines every half step and index lines every whole step from `contour_offset` 0.5 (tried 0 first: the heavy whole-step lines then are the terrace edges and read as the old staircase; at 0.5 the heavy lines run smooth half way up each rise and the thin whole-step ones mark the terrace edges), ticks every 22 px, and arrows only where `GreenView.steep_fall_at` (the steepest of the four one-sided slopes a quarter tile from the centre; the centred `fall_at` reads a two-step bank as 1) reaches 2 steps a tile. Answers the playtest's arrows question (24): arrows only on banks. Tunables in `scenes/green_view_tuning.tres` (`arrow_min_fall` and `arrow_length_steep` gone), design.md Green view rewritten. Tests: `tests/test_contours.gd` (7: ramp lines at their level at subdiv 1 and 3 and at the right x, chord midpoints within 0.05 steps on the hill, flat none, closed loop round the hill and clipped pieces, ticks downhill, levels, read-only) and two in `tests/test_green_view.gd` (steep-only arrows on the tiered bank and none on the contoured green; every contour vertex at its level, none on the flat green). `GreenViewShot.nine_hole` shows a playtest-nine green. Screenshots `docs/decisions/e3-s56-flat.png`, `-contoured.png`, `-contoured-gray.png`, `-tiered.png`, `-tiered-gray.png`, `-nine-3.png` looked at: flat has no lines, the contoured tilt reads as smooth diagonal index lines with the crown a closed loop, the tiered bank a dense band with arrows on both rows, readable in grayscale.

The isometric view draws flat tile tops (D17), so a line at a level between two tile centres falls on or near a riser: it is drawn on the ground as D17 draws everything on it (`tile_top_screen`), broken where it crosses a riser, or at its bilinear height, whichever reads better in screenshots; the story records which.

It redraws only when the course changes or the view moves, not per frame, and holds the frame budget at 0.175x on the playtest nine. Readable in grayscale and at every zoom step (D45).

  - Done: `course/topo_overlay.gd` (`TopoOverlay`, a Y-sorted child of CourseView, numbers in `course/topo_tuning.tres`): whole-step lines from `ContourTracer` (subdiv 2) over the visible tiles grown by `margin_tiles` 24, cut at tile edges into one `Row` node per sort key (a tile's top key + 0.25: after its top, edge cue and land hatch, before an object on it at + 0.5), each drawing its pieces and ticks in one `draw_multiline`, so hills in front hide lines behind them and the ball, golfers, flags, tags and aim draw over them. Traces only when the course changes (`CourseView.show_course`, a height change in `refresh_cells`) or the view leaves the traced window; lays out again only on a zoom step (widths and ticks are in screen px); a still or panning view does neither. `Main._apply_mode` sets `enabled` for Play beside `play_art`; `toggle_topo` (T, project.godot) hides and shows it, the choice held across modes. Placement, tried in screenshots on hole 4 at 1x (`PLAYTEST_TOPO` in `tests/fixtures/playtest_nine_shot.gd`): half-step levels at their own height draw along the middle of every riser face and over the back lip where a rise faces away, doubling the riser and edge cue and vanishing at 0.175x (`docs/decisions/e3-s57-option-level-half-1x.png`); half-step levels on the tile top sit exactly on tile edges and break everywhere (`-option-ground-half-1x.png`); whole-step levels from 0 on the tile top (D17), chosen: each line runs half a tile in from its terrace's falling edge, on the top of its edge tiles, which is also its own height, so it never breaks on one-step rises and reads as the outline of every terrace with ticks toward the drop, including rises facing away from the camera where no riser shows (down a bank of two steps or more the levels between sit on the tile under them, at the bank's top or foot). Perf on the playtest nine, `scripts/topo_perf.gd` (1280 x 720, vsync off): entering Play at 0.175x traces the whole map and lays out 671 rows once, 87 ms (trace 51 after reading node heights straight off the course instead of four `height_at` reads, 90 before; layout 30, as a zoom step's); idle at 0.175x 7.8 ms mean, 9.4 p95, 11.4 worst, against 5.6 / 6.5 / 7.2 hidden; panning the same and no trace or layout; at 0.5x 3.4 against 2.5. `tests/test_topo_overlay.gd` (6): off in Build and its key dead there, on in Play, T toggles and the choice holds; the line count equals the tracer's for the traced window, which encloses the visible one, at all four zooms; on one-step rises every piece lies on its level's terrace and down a bank only beside it; rows sort between the top and an object; no trace or layout over still frames or a pan, one layout on a zoom step; read-only. Screenshots `docs/decisions/e3-s57-0.175x.png` (whole course: hills read as stacked outlines), `-0.5x.png`, `-0.5x-gray.png`, `-1x.png`, `-2x.png` (hole 4's two-tier green) looked at; design.md Grid and terrain has the Contours line. Seen: at a terrace's inside corner the chord of the hugging curve cuts a quarter tile diagonally (subdiv 2), which reads as a short vertical tick on screen.

- [x] E3-S58 Draw the ground as smooth slopes. A hill reads as a hill, not a cliff (D50).
  - Note: (user, E3-S30 hand playtest, 2026-11-06) "when there is a hill, it should be a smooth slope, not a large cliff", whole course (D50). Replaces the flat tile tops and risers (D5) with a mesh over `HeightField.height_at`.
  - Accept: the course view draws the land as a mesh following `height_at` (each tile subdivided, the count in a `.tres`), textured from the surface sheets (labelled in Build, label-free in Play, E3-S55), lit by its slope (light direction and strength in the `.tres`) so a slope facing away from the camera reads as clearly as one facing it, readable in grayscale. Water lies flat at its level. Trees and other tile objects stand at the ground's height. The map's edge shows a side face down to the base. The picker returns the tile under the mouse on the sloped ground. Draws only when the course or view changes; holds the frame budget at 0.175x on the playtest nine. A one-step rise is a slope across the two half tiles either side of the edge; no risers are drawn.
  - Test: `tests/test_course_view.gd` (or a new `tests/test_terrain_mesh.gd`): mesh vertex heights equal `height_at` at sampled points, a flat course gives a flat mesh, a slope's shade differs from flat by the lighting rule, the picker picks the right tile on a slope. Screenshots of the playtest nine at 0.175x, 0.5x, 1x and 2x, a green close up, in Build and Play (`docs/decisions/e3-s58-*.png`) looked at.
  - Touches: `course/` (view only), `tests/`
  - Done: `course/terrain_mesh.gd` (`TerrainMesh`, numbers in `course/terrain_tuning.tres`) replaces the TileMap layers of tops, risers, edge cues and land hatch. A sloped tile (land whose clamped 3 x 3 heights differ) is a `subdiv` 2 grid with every vertex at `height_at` (4 looked the same and cost more), a flat tile one fan with the grid's points along edges it shares with a sloped tile (no T-junction cracks), water a flat fan at its level with a bank face (`side_color`) where land behind stands higher, and the map's two front edges a side face down to `base_steps` 0. Each tile's surface frame (Build labelled, Play label-free, `play_art` swaps the chunks' textures) is mapped onto its ground pulled in 1 px; unowned land is tinted and hatched the same way. Light per vertex `1 + light_strength * (n.x, n.y) . L` (`light_dir` (-1, -0.8) from behind the hills, `light_strength` 0.9, held to 0.55..1.25), times the E6-S18 height shade blended between steps, under the frame's headroom cap; flat ground is 1. Draw order: chunks of one depth row and `chunk_tiles` 32 tiles, Y-sorted at `Iso.depth_key`, so objects (`Iso.object_sort_key`) draw over their row and under the rows in front; an edit re-emits only the tiles within 2 of each changed tile (`Chunk.parts`) and rejoins their chunks. `TilePicker` walks the ray from one mesh grid line to the next and meets the drawn triangles exactly (`TerrainMesh.ground_in`), faces included; `SwingControl` aims at the picked ground point. `Iso.riser_sort_key`, `CourseView.riser_frame`, `edge_cue_frame`, `top_alt` and the layer getters are gone; `CourseView.chunks`, `chunk_at` and `terrain_digest` let tests read what is drawn. Riser and edge cue sheets are no longer drawn (kept for the tile preview; specs say so). Tests: `tests/test_terrain_mesh.gd` (16: vertices at `height_at` on the stress fixture, drawn points are their projection, flat course flat with faces only on the front edges, a one-step rise is a ramp with no riser, no cracks between any two neighbours, water flat with its bank, the lighting rule and its band and cap, the height shade blend, chunk order and object keys, an edit equals a fresh draw, the picker on a ramp, behind a hill, on a side face, and against the triangles as drawn on a random 8 x 8 with water); `tests/test_course_view.gd`, `test_builder.gd`, `test_issue_marker.gd`, `test_iso_projection.gd`, `test_land_hud.gd`, `test_builder_holes.gd`, `test_guest_view.gd` rewritten for the mesh. Perf on the playtest nine (`scripts/terrain_perf.gd`, 1280 x 720, vsync off; terraces before in brackets): whole draw 379 ms (106), redraw after a 1 x 1 raise 1.8 ms (0.06), 5 x 5 5.3 ms (0.45); idle Build 2.6 / 1.3 / 0.9 ms at 0.175x / 0.5x / 1x (5.0 / 2.8 / 1.8), Play contours hidden 2.8 / 1.3 / 0.9 (5.8 / 2.6 / 1.7), shown 4.5 / 2.1 / 1.3 (8.1 / 3.5 / 2.4). Screenshots `docs/decisions/e3-s58-play-0.175x.png`, `-play-0.5x.png`, `-play-0.5x-gray.png`, `-play-1x.png`, `-play-2x-green.png`, `-build-0.175x.png`, `-build-0.5x.png`, `-build-green-1x.png`, `-build-green-2x.png` and `-green-view.png` (the green view panel draws its own tiles, unchanged) looked at: hills read as slopes, away-facing slopes lighter and camera-facing darker, readable in grayscale. Left for E3-S59: everything on the ground (ball, aim, golfers, flags, markers, issue boxes, contours, the Build brush outline) is still drawn on the tile top (D17), so on a slope it floats over or sinks into the drawn ground by up to half a step; the contours show it most.

Hole markers stand at their tile's centre, where the ground is the tile's own height, the tee's two blocks each on the ground under it; the issue diamond and the Build brush and parcel outlines follow the ground round the tile (`TerrainMesh.ring`, `ground_in`); `IssueMarker.covered` and `LandBoundary.covered` read the drawn ground (`TilePicker.hidden`: something drawn higher up the ray); the land line lies on the ground in half-tile pieces (the ground bends at a tile's centre line), on the higher side at a water bank, and `AimView` builds it again only when the course's revision changes (a ray cast per piece, about 0.3 ms each headless). Contours (`TopoOverlay`) are drawn at their own height on the ground, unbroken, each tick's foot on the ground, none over water, Y-sorted at their row's key + 0.25 (`TopoTuning.placement` gone; `Iso.top_sort_key` gone). Offset, tried in screenshots on hole 4 at 1x: 0 (whole steps) now runs along the top edge of each slope (`docs/decisions/e3-s59-option-contours-whole-1x.png`); 0.5, chosen, runs half way up each slope, on the shaded band it marks. Perf (`scripts/topo_perf.gd`): entering Play at 0.175x 84 ms (trace 49, layout 23; 210 rows against 671), idle 3.7 ms at 0.175x against 2.8 hidden, 2.0 against 1.2 at 0.5x. Tests: `tests/test_shot_view.gd` (a ball held on a green slope, set down, and rolling down a bank drawn at `height_at` every tick with no step over 8 px a tick; water at its level), new `tests/test_aim_view.gd` (3: the marker on a slope at `height_at` where the tile top would be off, the water cue at the water's level, a putt's marker), `tests/test_swing.gd` (each putt dot on the ground), `tests/test_topo_overlay.gd` (every drawn point on the ground at its level, unbroken chords, none over water, row keys), `tests/test_guest_view.gd` (feet at `height_at`), `tests/test_shot_prediction.gd`, `test_issue_marker.gd` (the foot hidden only once the slope in front climbs past the line of sight; the diamond on the ground), `test_ball_land.gd` (the land line on the ground). Screenshots looked at: `docs/decisions/e3-s59-putt-2x.png` (a putt at rest across the sloped green with its dots), `-bank-2x.png` and `-bank-1x.png` (a ball rolling down a bank, on the slope), `-behind-2x.png` (`tests/fixtures/ground_things_shot.tscn`: a ridge's back slope hides a golfer's legs and the player's ball behind it, its head shows; the golfer on top stands whole), `-contours-0.175x.png`, `-0.5x.png`, `-0.5x-gray.png`, `-1x.png`, `-2x.png`. Seen: on a twisted bilinear square the mesh's two triangles and the bilinear ground differ a little between grid points (under a quarter step on the stress fixture), so a ball there can sit up to a few px off the drawn triangle; the land line steps at a water bank's corner.

- [x] E3-S61 Keep a drag on doglegs inside the frame. The breaking drag's frames are 52 ms on PlaytestNine.
  - Note: (found in E3-S33) `scripts/nine_holes_perf.gd -- fixture=doglegs`: a drag of rough through every cup holds `drag_to` frames median 52 ms (p95 94) and its release and undo frames median 16.6 to 16.9, against 9 to 12 on the bare nine. `CourseBuilder._close_footprints` measures every open hole's line of play again after each paint that turns a tile to or from fairway (`_line_now` keeps it otherwise, E3-S33), and a dogleg's `CourseValidator.turn_point` floods its fairway to the cup; `check_course` is 4.3 ms there against 2.4.
  - Accept: on `fixture=doglegs` the breaking drag's `drag_to`, release and undo frames stay under 16.7 ms at p95 with guests out, for example by measuring a hole's line again only when a fairway write could reach its turn search, or once per frame of a stroke.

  - Done 2026-11-08: measured first (a headless profile of the breaking drag in the main scene, each signal handler timed): 344 of a stroke's 407 ms was not the builder but `GuestSim._on_tiles_changed`, `ReactionFeed.cells_edited` measuring every hole's line of play (a dogleg's turn search) on every paint. Now `GuestSim` gathers a drag's tiles and hands them to the feed once a frame (deferred) and at every check (`_flush_cells`), testing each slot's line now (`CourseBuilder.lines_now`, kept) and its line before the drag (`slot_lines`), so a drag that moves or removes a turn still clears the hole. `CourseValidator.turn_point` names the tiles it read (`reads`: the disc, the ring, every tile the fairway walk looked at), and the builder keeps each hole's line until a fairway write lands on one of them (one hole's fairway no longer remeasures the others), hands the kept turns to `check_slots` (`known_turns`), and a stroke's undo puts back the lines kept when it began. `scripts/nine_holes_perf.gd` now holds the main scene's day in the Build phases that run the script's own (since E3-S62 they ran both, one untimed). On `fixture=doglegs` (load 0.7 to 1.4, `docs/decisions/e3-s61-doglegs-perf.txt`), before / after: the headless stroke 41.9 / 8.4 ms; `drag_to` frames median 53.6 / 10.0 (p95 95.3 / 11.8), call 47.4 / 2.3; release frame median 20.7 / 19.5 (p95 29.4 / 22.6), call 10.4 / 8.1; undo frame 19.5 / 17.5 (p95 24.6 / 20.4), call 12.5 / 9.4. So the `drag_to` frames meet the Accept; the release and undo frames do not: what is left there is the check (`check_slots`' rules 2.3 ms, the issue boxes 1.8), the guests' work (4.5 ms a frame while the terrain keeps changing, likely `HoleChecks` reading `terrain_key` and the hazard grid again after every edit) and the draw after the release; filed as E3-S74. Tests: `tests/test_builder_holes.gd` `test_a_dogleg_whose_turn_an_edit_moves_still_closes` (a far fairway write keeps the dogleg's line, fairway in the landing zone off its footprint makes it straight and closes it, undo brings both back) and `test_a_turn_reads_nothing_but_the_tiles_it_names` (fairway on every tile it did not read leaves the turn); `tests/test_feed.gd` `test_a_drag_that_brings_back_a_doglegs_turn_clears_its_tally` (read at the frame's end, against the line before the drag). `docs/design.md` Plot.

The `drag_to` frames are 10 ms (E3-S61).

  - Done 2026-11-08: profiled first (the main scene's breaking drag on doglegs, each builder signal handler and the view's pieces timed): the guests were not it. `HoleChecks` measured a hole in 1 frame of about 500 (no guest finishes a hole between the drag and its undo); the 4.5 ms of guest work a frame is their Ball work, the same in a frame with no edit. The release call was the view: nine `IssueMarker.place`s (0.3 ms each: the foot's ray and two rings on the ground), their layout 1.7 ms, and `refresh_holes` freeing and rebuilding all eighteen hole markers 1.6 ms; the undo call was `refresh_cells` placing the nine issue boxes again (2.5 ms) just before the check freed them, and `refresh_holes` again. Now: `refresh_cells` places a marker again only when a height or water within a tile of it moved (an issue box's foot read again when one moved up to 6 tiles in front, `MARK_FRONT`), so a surface-only batch leaves them standing; `refresh_holes` keeps a marker it would draw the same and lays the numbers out only when one was made or removed; a box a check drops is kept hidden as its tile's spare (`_spare_issues`, at most 32, freed when a shape change reaches it) and filled again when a box is wanted there; `TilePicker.cast` reads `TerrainTuning` once (`load` cost 0.08 ms a cast). And as filed: `CourseData` counts height, per-surface and land changes (`height_revision`, `surface_revision(s)`, `land_revision`), `HoleChecks.terrain_key` keys on the heights, the four hazard surfaces and land (no hash of the map), and `GuestWalk.Cache` keeps the cart path searches until a cart path tile changes (any write dropped them and the 0.9 ms path grid). On `fixture=doglegs` (load 1.0 to 1.4, `docs/decisions/e3-s74-doglegs-perf.txt`), before / after: release frame median 20.0 / 14.6 ms (p95 22.1 / 15.9), call 8.4 / 3.3; undo frame 18.5 / 14.0 (p95 20.9 / 15.6), call 9.9 / 5.3; `drag_to` frames unchanged (10.1 / 10.2). The p95 is the second worst of 40 and moves about 1.5 ms run to run (other runs during the work: release 16.0 to 16.3, undo 15.8 to 17.3, one release run at 20.3 from guest frames), so the Accept holds with little room: what is left is the guests' Ball work (4.5 to 5.7 ms) and the engine's frame (about 4 ms, as idle). Bare nine: release frame 14.0 / 9.1 (p95 15.3 to 17.0 / 11.2), undo 12.2 / 8.6. The gated guest days' statics fell from about 117 holes measured a day to 9, and their frames over 10 ms headless (alternating with main, 3 runs each) from 3, 3, 4 to 1, 1, 1; but with a display the bare nine's D52 gate still failed 3 runs of 3 on the final code (2 to 3 gated frames, worst 10.7 to 13.2; main passed 2 of 2 in the same session, 1 and 2 frames): those frames are Ball work and measure no hole, so this does not steady the gate (left open, not filed). `tests/test_guest_brain.gd`'s walled day now paints bunker and rough by turns, so its edits still make the statics be measured again (fairway over rough keeps them). Tests: `tests/test_reactions.gd` `test_statics_are_kept_through_an_edit_that_changes_no_hazard_height_or_land`, `tests/test_course_data.gd` `test_shape_counters_move_only_on_what_they_count`, `tests/test_course_view.gd` `test_refresh_holes_keeps_the_markers_it_would_draw_the_same`, `test_a_surface_edit_leaves_the_markers_standing`, `test_a_dropped_issue_box_is_filled_again_while_its_ground_stands`, `tests/test_guest_brain.gd` (a walk's search kept through edits off the path), and `tests/test_hole_list.gd` (a changed box on the same tile is filled again, not rebuilt).

On `fixture=doglegs` every run after E3-S74 failed (2 to 6 frames). The frames over the line are the guests' Ball work, not hole statics. E3-S70's turn measurement (`GuestHole.turn_of`) at a hole's start is unbudgeted. Not yet known whether this is noise near the line, a display-only cost, or a regression.
  - Accept: an A/B on a quiet machine (main before E3-S74 against now, at least 5 display runs each, bare nine and doglegs) says which it is; then the gate passes at least 4 runs in 5 on both fixtures without loosening D52's thresholds, or a `/decision` amends D52 with the measured reason.

  - Done: the A/B (display, quiet, alternating, 5 runs a side a fixture; `docs/decisions/e3-s75-gate-perf.txt`) says noise near the line, not a regression nor a display-only cost: bed8a77 passed 3 of 5 on the bare nine and 3 of 5 on doglegs (10 and 14 gated frames), main 4 of 5 and 1 of 5 (10 and 17; one run a side went past load 4 and counts as failed). Most gated frames come from the lattice and flooded days, which play the bare nine's land whichever fixture is run; none ran a step, so E3-S70's `turn_of` is not in them, and the Ball tick cost is the same on E3-S34, before E3-S70, bed8a77 and main (26.0 to 26.4 us, headless whole days). A scratch profile found each gated frame to be a full frame of Ball work (about 7 ms) in which one ordinary tick took 1 to 5 ms; a control of plain GDScript arithmetic stalls the same way (26 stalls of 1 to 5.3 ms in 60 s). So the fix makes a full frame cheaper, not the gate looser: `CourseData.get_height`, `get_surface` and `is_owned` spell out their bounds check and index (the same reads, the same days tick for tick), and a tick costs 20.6 us (-21%). After: 4 of 5 pass on the bare nine (3 gated frames; the one fail had 3, worst 13.3) and 5 of 5 on doglegs (2). D52 and `frame_guest_ms_max` unchanged. ## E4: Economy — done

Ships as 0.5.0 (D19). Gives building a budget and reactions a consequence. Serves pillar 3, and stays deliberately thin: one input, one output.

Done when: a player starts with $5,000, funds the course from green fees, and can save, quit and pick up where they left off.

  - Note: (found in E6 review) once paint has a cost, set `tree_tile_price` and `water_tile_price` in `data/land.json` against what clearing a tree or filling a pond costs, then rerun `/balance land`. Today trees and water take $52 off a parcel on average and $204 at most, and relief sets the price (E6-S8).
  - Accept: each item below has a line in `docs/design.md`, and a `/decision` where it is a real choice.
  - Test: `scripts/sim.gd --table economy` runs and prints measured values against the target.
  - Touches: `docs/`, `scripts/`
  - Settles:
    - Hole costs. The design doc says a basic par 3 is about $1,500 and a par 5 with hazards about $6,000. At the table's rates, a 5x5 green and 2x2 tee alone are $3,320. A 400-yard fairway 6 tiles wide is $19,200. Either tile costs, tile scale or hole budgets must move.
    - Whether raising and lowering terrain costs money, and what features cost.
    - Refunds when a tile is repainted or an edit undone.
    - How E6's land budget folds into the one balance, and whether parcel prices change once fees exist (D19).
    - Arrivals per day by rating.
    - Arrivals curve for fees between free and the ceiling, since only "at the ceiling" is defined.
    - Rating points per reaction type, and the starting rating before 50 reactions exist.

Numbers in `data/economy.json`, read by `course/economy_tuning.gd` (`EconomyTuning`; a reader only, `tests/test_economy_tuning.gd`), and `data/land.json` (`LandPrices.price_scale`, live now: a flat parcel is $600, so until E4-S2 the $5,000 buys 8 to 13 parcels). `docs/design.md` Economy and time, Land and Balance targets carry every number with its source. `/balance economy` prints the chosen economy first ("chosen:" rows, the only ones with targets: the 3-star run opens its nine on day 28 with 3 stars after 11 days of stall, lowest balance $1,037, every target hit; PlaytestNine's measured mix after E4-S9 does the same, day 28, scale nearest day 30 1.04) and then the options pass without targets; `/balance land` reran with every target hit (the wettest 10% of parcels now sit at the $300 floor, recorded, not a target). E4-S5's Note is answered (D58). The Claude Docs design doc's cost table, hole prices and fee ceiling now disagree with D56 to D58 and need mirroring.

- [x] E5-S4 Add audio. Sound that serves readability.

  - Done: (user: generate the sounds in code) `scripts/gen_audio.py` (Python stdlib, sfxr-style, deterministic: a rerun writes the same bytes) writes 19 WAVs into `audio/`: a strike per lie (`strike_tee`, `_fairway`, `_rough`, `_bunker`, `_cart_path`, `putt`), a touchdown per surface (`land_fairway` for fairway and tee, `_rough`, `_green`, `_bunker`, `_cart_path`, `splash`, `trees`, `oob`), `cup`, the `praise` and `complaint` stings, a 12 s `ambient` wind-and-birds bed and an 8 s `music` loop (both loop through a WAV `smpl` chunk the importer reads). `audio/LICENSE.md` records that all are original and generated. Buses Master, Music, Ambient, SFX (`audio/bus_layout.tres`, set in project.godot). `audio/game_audio.gd` (`GameAudio`, a node on the main scene, bound in `Main._ready`) listens to `SwingControl.struck` (the lie), `ShotView.landed` (the surface), polls the ball for trees, a water or OB stop and a holed ball, plays a sting per praise or complaint in the feed (at most one per `sting_min_gap_s`), and drops the music by `music_swing_db` from a swing's first click until its ball rests. Tunables in `audio/audio_tuning.tres` (docs/design.md Audio). Volume settings: F2 opens a keyboard-focused sound panel (`scenes/audio_panel.gd`, a slider per bus, Escape closes), saved in `user://audio_settings.cfg` (`AudioSettings`).   - Note: E5-S2/S3 added 16 variants per surface, an 80 px tree on every trees tile (about 1,750 on the nine) and decor; no perf run since.
  - Accept: `scripts/nine_holes_perf.gd` on the bare nine and `fixture=doglegs`, with a display on a quiet machine (D52): the gate passes, and the frames E3's perf stories hold (Build and Play at 0.175x with a full field, the drag and undo frames of E3-S16 and E3-S74) are where they were before E5 or under 16.7 ms at p95. Any fix keeps the look: the 0.175x and 1x screenshots unchanged.
  - Test: `tests/test_sprites.gd` `test_tree_edits_redraw_only_what_they_change_and_match_a_fresh_view`, `test_a_trees_foot_is_the_drawn_ground_at_its_tile_centre`; the perf runs in `docs/decisions/e5-s15-perf.txt`.
  - Touches: `course/`, `tests/`, `docs/`
  - Done: measured first (dc8112a, quiet machine): the far zoom held (bare nine Build 0.175x median 8.56 / p95 12.16 ms, one frame of 18.8; Play 0.175x 8.77 / 11.27; doglegs 8.22 / 10.54 and 8.78 / 10.99), but every drag and undo frame missed: bare nine breaking `drag_to` frames 30.6 / 87.9 ms, its undo 28.9 / 31.7, off-line `drag_to` 34.3 / 52.2, its undo 36.0 / 42.1; doglegs breaking `drag_to` 35.2 / 47.0, undo 43.0 / 48.0 (E3-S62: 8 to 13 ms). Cause: `TreeView.refresh_cells` rebuilt every depth row within 2 of each edited tile, and a drag emits one batch per brush step, so a `drag_to` rebuilt a few hundred rows (0.17 ms each). Fix: trees redraw tile by tile (`TreeView` keeps each tile's height and trees bit, and a Row per depth row and height holds its trees by tile), only where a tile turned to or from trees or a trees tile's height moved; a tree's foot is its tile's own height, which is the drawn ground at a land tile's centre (the new test holds it to `TerrainMesh.ground_at`), so no neighbour is read; the decor redraws only when a batch moved a height or water. After (three bare-nine runs, one doglegs; load 0.7 to 2.9 at the start): bare nine Build 0.175x 7.8 to 7.9 / 10.3 to 10.6 (worst 11.6 to 12.7), Play 0.175x 8.2 to 8.6 / 10.7 to 11.4, Play 0.5x 6.4 to 6.5 / 8.6 to 8.9, breaking `drag_to` 7.6 to 7.9 / 9.5 to 10.6, its undo 8.3 to 8.7 / 9.8 to 12.0, off-line `drag_to` 9.9 to 10.3 / 12.6 to 13.4, its undo 13.2 to 15.1 / 15.5 to 18.6 (where it ran before E5: E3-S62 15.4 to 17.2; the 585-tile undo puts back 78 trees, 0.8 ms); doglegs Build 0.175x 8.44 / 11.29, Play 0.175x 9.28 / 11.90, breaking `drag_to` 8.04 / 10.82, undo 13.06 / 14.30. D52 gate: no run fails (one doglegs run under an outside build's load 52 printed the WARNING and was retaken). Screenshots `tests/fixtures/nine_hazards_shot.tscn` (NINE_AT=tee) at 0.175x and 1x before and after are pixel-identical (`magick compare -metric AE` 0). `scripts/run_tests.sh` 1150/1150, `scripts/playtest.sh` PASS.
