- [x] E7-S4 Make skip to morning quick. A busy day skips in a few seconds.
  - Note: (from E4-S3) a busy day on the playtest nine is about 530,000 units of ball work and skips in 11 to 15 s, with the HUD still moving. It must still end exactly as the day run at any speed (E4-S3), and save/load replays must still match (E4-S7, E4-S11).
  - Accept: skipping a full 24-guest day on PlaytestNine takes under 3 s of real time on the reference machine, with the screen responsive (a progress line, no frozen window), and the same end state as the played day.
  - Test: `tests/test_day_clock.gd`; the timing in the commit.
  - Touches: `guests/`, `scenes/`, `tests/`
  - Done 2026-11-11 (D63): profiled first: 98% of a skip was Ball work, about 21 us a tick headless, and a full 24-guest day on PlaytestNine is about 820,000 ticks (18 s of work; 24.5 s in the window at 1,000 ticks a frame). The cost was GDScript calls (HeightField and CourseData reads, the tick's helpers), and calls from several threads at once queue on each other in the engine, while plain sums scale. Fix, three parts, every ball and day the same bit for bit: `HeightField` reads the course's packed tiles (`CourseData.packed_tiles`, `packed_owned`, taken again when `revision` moves); `Ball.run(max_ticks)` spells the whole tick out (flight and roll, HeightField's reads, felt_gradient, crease_slope, meet_ground, downhill_share, captures) on the ball's own copies of the tiles, BallParams and surface rows, with no GDScript call per tick (rare paths call out), and GuestHole, GuestPlan and PuttAim step their balls through it (4.3 us a tick); a skip frame (`GuestBrain.advance_skip`) runs whole seconds, each served at once with the guests side by side on WorkerThreadPool (`serve_second`: a batch is the guests whose own budgets fit in what the sim's cap leaves, so none is cut by another), the steps on the main thread, until `skip_frame_ms` 28 (new, replaces `skip_frame_ticks`; a second costing what the last did is not started past it; `skip_steps_per_frame` stays 60, so a quiet stretch shows the clock a minute a frame and `scripts/e4_playtest.gd` still catches day 3's last minute); while the frame is drawn the next batch runs on the threads (`serve_ahead` at `RenderingServer.frame_pre_draw`, `settle` at `frame_post_draw` and before anything else touches the guests), and GuestView takes the golfers off the course while it skips (the speed panel's "skipping" line is the progress line; they are drawn again where the skip ends or is stopped). CourseData is still written only by the builder on the main thread, and only read by guests. Timings (`scripts/skip_perf.gd`, seed 1, 24 guests, the window at 60 Hz, about 30 fps while skipping, longest frame 36 to 38 ms): Build 2.61 to 2.65 s at load 3.9 (2.67 to 2.85 at load 7 to 10), Play 2.39 to 2.45 s, headless 2.40 s; before, 24.51 s (931 frames) in the window, 17.9 s for the brain alone, which now takes 3.8 s on one thread. The day skipped ends as the brain's own run_day (every guest's records and ticks). Tests: `tests/test_ball_kernel.gd` (HeightField's reads and the ball's own against the old code kept as `tests/fixtures/reference_field.gd` and `reference_ball.gd`, over thousands of points on the nine and small clamped maps; 350 shots and putts on the nine tick for tick, run in random chunks; a write mid-roll; run against step), `tests/test_day_clock.gd` (a skip frame runs whole seconds within its time; threads and serve_ahead serve 150 s of the 24-guest nine as one thread does; serve_ahead's batch is counted before a step reads the guests; a full 24-guest day on the nine skips in under 3 s headless, timed; the golfers leave while it skips and come back where it stops), `tests/test_guest_tuning.gd`. `bash scripts/run_tests.sh` and `--full`, `scripts/playtest.sh` and `scripts/e4_playtest.gd --fixed-fps 60 -- --seed 1` pass (day 4 on the nine still ends on $9,605, the replay agreeing). `docs/design.md` Economy and time.

Profiled the call (585 tiles, 78 trees): tile writes 1.95 ms, the tiles_changed handlers 1.79 (trees 0.84, the markers' shape loop 0.43, the ground's sort and drain 0.45), the validator rerun 1.65, checked 0.70. Cause: per-tile work. Every tile of the undo went through `_set_tile`, which on a fairway flip searches every kept line's reads (E3-S61) though undo then puts the lines and reads back whole from the step; and `TreeView.refresh_cells`, `CourseView.refresh_cells` and `CourseView._classify` read each tile through three or four GDScript calls. Fix: `CourseBuilder.undo` writes a step that kept its lines through `_write_tile` (`_set_tile` less the search), and the three loops read `CourseData.packed_tiles()` in place. After (three runs): that frame's p95 11.61, 11.60, 11.34 ms (median 9.1 to 9.4), the undo call 4.0 to 4.1 ms median; every other drag and undo row holds or improves (breaking drag_to 6.5 to 6.8 / 8.6 to 9.0, its release 8.4 to 8.6 / 11.1 to 11.5, its undo 7.3 to 7.4 / 9.0 to 11.5, off-line drag_to 7.9 to 8.0 / 9.4 to 9.6; Build and Play at 0.175x p95 6.8 to 8.2). D52 gate: 0 gated frames on all six runs, no WARNING. ## E9: Cart paths matter — done

Ships as the release candidate after E8's (rc4). Today a cart path costs $5 a tile, makes the ball bounce and run, and never helps a guest: guests walk 40 yd a second over any tile, so a path can only lengthen a walk (E3-S4). The user chose (2026-11-11) SimGolf's model: guests walk faster on paths, and long walks without one draw complaints.

Done when: on a course with a far tee, laying a cart path between the green and the next tee measurably cuts the walk, its pace complaints and the "long walk" comments, and the reaction targets still hold.

- [x] E9-S1 Walk faster on cart paths. A path is the quick way round.
  - Note: needs a `/decision` (it amends E3-S4's walking rule and D60's clubhouse walks). Guests walk at a base speed over rough, fairway, tee and green, faster on a cart path, and slower through trees, bunkers, water and up steep ground (each a tunable in `data/guests.json`). `GuestWalk.route` keeps its search but chooses the quickest route by time, not the shortest by distance, so a path is taken when it saves time; the detour cap becomes a time cap. Walk time feeds pace as today.
  - Accept: a guest's walk over a path is faster than the same distance over rough by the tuned factor; a walk that a path makes quicker uses it, one that it makes slower does not; skip, save/load replays and the 24-guest day still finish (`scripts/guest_day.gd`); `/balance reactions` hits every target, retuned if needed.

  - Touches: `guests/`, `data/`, `tests/`
  - Done 2026-11-11 (D65): `GuestWalk` walks by time. `time_pieces` cuts a walk's line at every tile edge and sums each piece at `GuestTuning.walk_speed` of its tile (`walk_yd_per_s` 40 times `walk_surface_speed`: cart path 1.5, bunker 0.6, trees 0.6, water 0.75, 1 elsewhere) plus `walk_climb_s_per_step` 0.15 s a height step climbed tile to tile; a walk keeps `duration`, `straight_s` (the straight line's time) and `elapsed`, and `advance` / `point_at_time` replace `move` / `point_at`. `route` takes the cart path's route only when quicker than the straight walk; the search's bound is that time (legs at the top speed, steps at path speed), and `cart_path_max_detour` is retired. The brain's `_walk` and `stands_at_impact` are by time; walking stays on its main-thread step (D63). Pace's walk is judged by its time (`walk_base_s` + `walk_per_patience_s` x patience, `Guest.HoleRecord.walk_s`), so a path never fails pace by its yards; GuestView's sidestep moves at `walk_top_yd_per_s`. Skip to morning (`scripts/skip_perf.gd`, headless, load 3): 2.61 s, the same day as the brain's. A test's walk of 202 yd from a far clubhouse takes 5.06 s over rough and 3.70 s along a 219 yd path (`test_a_cart_path_from_a_far_clubhouse_cuts_the_walk_out_and_its_pace`).   - Note: the walk check (D65, E9-S2) counts a walk as served only when a cart path starts within `cart_path_reach_yd` 25 yd of the cup it walks from, ends within 25 yd of the next tee, covers `walk_path_share` 0.5 of the walk, and is quicker; none of that shows, and complaints given before the path stay in the hole's tag. The user chose all four: (1) with the Cart path tool in hand, draw every walk (each open hole's green to the next open tee, and the clubhouse door to the first tee) as the route guests would take, red when it would draw a complaint and green when a path serves it, labelled with its time and, when red, the reason ("path stops 32 yd short of the 5th tee", "no path within reach of the 4th green", "path covers 30% of the walk", "path is slower than walking across"); (2) the complaint names the same reason; (3) laying or removing cart path that a walk can reach clears that walk's complaints from the hole's tag and tally (E3-S17's clearing, the walk check only), so the next guests' verdict shows; (4) loosen the rule: `cart_path_reach_yd` to about 40 and drop the half-the-walk share (a path that serves the walk and is quicker counts), recorded as a `/decision` amending D65, with `/balance reactions` rerun and its targets kept.
  - Accept: on a test course, a path that stops short of a tee shows a red walk with the gap in yards and draws a complaint naming it; extending it turns the walk green and clears the old walk complaints; the overlay is hidden in Play and costs no frame time when the tool is not in hand (D52); every reaction target still passes.
  - Test: `tests/test_guest_walk.gd`, `tests/test_reactions.gd`, `tests/test_walk_overlay.gd`; screenshots of red and green walks; the balance output in the commit.
  - Touches: `guests/`, `scenes/`, `data/`, `tests/`
  - Done 2026-11-12 (D68): one reason function, `GuestWalk.why` (`GuestWalk.Reason`: "no cart path", "no path within reach of the 4th green", "path stops N yd short of the 5th tee" or "of the 4th green", "path is slower than walking across"), read by the complaint ("Hole 5: a long walk from the 4th green to the 5th tee, 240 yd: path stops 32 yd short of the 5th tee."; `GuestBrain` asks it at tee-off of a walk with no path over the least patient guest's limit; a save keeps it) and by the Build overlay (`scenes/walk_overlay.gd` `WalkOverlay`, a BuildTool child): with the Cart path brush in hand every walk of `GuestWalk.round_walks` is drawn on the ground as guests walk it, green "2.2 s on the path", red "2.8 s walk" over the reason with a dashed line across a short path's gap, chalk when short; measured on a course, hole or clubhouse change, idle (not processing) without the brush, hidden in Play; colours in `course/builder_tuning.tres` `walk_*`. Clearing: tiles that turned to or from cart path (`GuestSim` diffs its path grid on each flush) clear the failed-walk reactions about each hole whose walk they can reach (`GuestWalk.path_edit_reaches`, `ReactionFeed.walks_edited` / `clear_walk`, lines dimmed), and a guest that teed off before the edit gives its walk complaint uncounted (`GuestBrain.walked_to`). Rule: `cart_path_reach_yd` 40 (25), `walk_path_share` retired (a walk the route takes along a path passes); the story's "path covers 30% of the walk" reason went with the share. Balance: `/balance reactions --runs 50 --seed 1` the same rows bit for bit before and after, every target hit (D65's walk rows 0.022 and 0.000, D61's dogleg +0.133), no walk time retuned (`docs/decisions/e12-s1-balance-reactions.txt`). `scripts/guest_day.gd arrivals=24 quick` seeds 1 to 5: every group finished nine, latest finish 471/478/521/442/466 s (dusk 540), the same as before the story (the nine has no cart path); the skill-1 foursome sweep is short in one slot on day 1 and three on day 3, also the same before this story (E5-S12 recorded every slot holding; something between then and D67 moved it). Docs: `docs/how-to-play.md` cart path line ($6, the overlay, 40 yd, the clearing). Tests: `tests/test_walk_overlay.gd` (shown only with the brush, idle otherwise, never in Play; red "no cart path"; a path stopping short shows "path stops 19 yd short of the 2nd tee" and the guests' complaints name the same; extended it turns green; short of the green; measured once a change; walk-only clearing on laying and taking up path, through GuestSim, and an in-flight walk complaint uncounted), `tests/test_guest_walk.gd` (40 yd, every reason, the cache, `path_edit_reaches`, `round_walks` against the brain), `tests/test_reactions.gd` (the reason in the complaint and its save; a part-path walk passes), `tests/test_hole_list.gd`, `tests/test_playtest_nine.gd`. Screenshots looked at: `docs/decisions/e12-s1-walks-red-1x.png`, `-red-0.5x.png`, `-green-1x.png`, `-green-0.5x.png` (labels in an ink box beside the line so the line and the gap's dot are never under the words).
  - Open: red means the least patient guest (patience 1, 2.0 s, 80 yd) would complain, so a 2.8 s walk most guests accept still shows red; a "how many complain" figure could soften it. A label near the map's edge or under the HUD is not moved into view.

## Icebox

Things worth doing that are out of the MVP. Promote only through `/decision`, which must say what it delays to pay for them. Feel problems from playtests go at the top with the pillar named.

- Course thumbnail in exported files (was part of E5-S5, moved 2026-11-10 by D60 to pay for the clubhouse). Pillar 3.
- Tee in an L's corner (was E3-S42, moved 2026-11-08). The framing never tries a long hole's tee in the L's corner of the free view. Measured 2026-11-07: over 72 directions, 4 lengths and both list states the corner never showed more than 2 points more of the tee-to-cup line than the better strip, since each strip's framing already clamps the tee into the elbow. Reopen if the HUD changes shape (a left panel or a bottom panel that does not span the view). Pillar 2.
- Land corridor width and D29 (found in E6 review). Is a one-parcel (80 yd) wide corridor right for a par 4? On the E6-S8 lanes 29% of swept tee strokes within 60 degrees of the line ended OB at the land line. D29's strict reading (OB on a roll over the line even if the ball would have come back) changed 1 of 12,628 swept strokes; recheck if players raise steep ground beside the line. For the user's hand playtest. Pillar 2.
- Off-green roll feel after E6-S10 (found in E6 review). Fairway and tee roll about 12% shorter on flat ground, balls run faster down off-green slopes, and a swing across a valley floor dies sooner. For the user's hand playtest. Pillar 2.
- Putt marker while a solve runs (found in E6 review). After a new stroke or a switch to the putter no marker shows until the first putt rests (up to 2 to 3 frames on a steep bank), a cold steep solve spreads over about 1 s, and a marker that trails a sweep gives no sign it is for an older aim (a fade or dash could). Accuracy is measured only on straight two-steps-per-tile risers, not curved or mixed banks. Pillar 2.
- Default club on short holes (found in playtest 2026-11-03). The tee defaults to the longest legal club, so on a 160 yd par 3 the driver's marker flies past the cup and out of bounds. Proposed: default to the longest club whose marker stays short of the cup. Pillar 2.
- Blocked-club and hazard cues (found in playtest 2026-11-03). The blocked ring (D20), the red landing cross at the tree scatter point, or the 0.8 s `hazard_show_time` pause read as unclear; a splash cue was the open idea. Ask which before fixing. Pillar 2.
- Campaign plots and goals
- Unlocks by rating
- Tournament mode
- Second plot
- Weather, including wind. Wind was cut at E2 planning (user, 2026-11-03; E2-S1 records the /decision). If promoted, the smallest version is one fixed vector per hole that shifts the aim marker and shows on the Play toolbar. Pillar 4.
- Staff
- Clubhouse
- Camera rotation
- Online course sharing
- Multiplayer
- Mobile
- One-click power-then-release swing. The A/B prototype from the original E2-S9 was dropped at E2 planning. Promote it through `/decision` only if the E2-S9 playtest finds that the three-click bar, at its speed setting, fails checklist item 2. Pillar 2.
- OB stakes as placed boundary objects, beyond painted `oob` ground. Needs a new CourseData field and course format version 2 with a `save.gd` migration. Pillar 4.
- Seed on the HUD (found in E6 review). New course always picks a random seed; `Main.land_seed` holds it if the player should see it or type one in.
- More varied generated maps (found in E6 review). With the bisected radius factor, high ground is exactly 15.0% on every seed; a jitter on the target would vary maps more (`/balance`).
- No ball on a course with no hole (found in E6 review). Play could show only the "No hole" panel instead of placing a ball; changes the sandbox and the fixture-course tests.
- Guests read break on putts (found in E3 review). Guests aim every putt at the cup and nobody reads break in the MVP (`docs/design.md`, D41), so even a noise-free guest misses a breaking putt by the break. A putt line search for guests would need a `/decision`. E3-S24's rates (`/balance reactions --runs 50 --seed 1`, 10 and 15 yd, three-or-more putts) say break is not what causes three-putts: a noise-free guest never three-putts the sloped or tiered green but two-putts 75% and 50% of the time by the unread break, and noisy guests three-putt about as often on the flat green (skills 1/4/7/10: 61/53/36/19%) as on the sloped one (69/59/47/28%) or the tiered one (70/56/41/19%). Fix putt noise first ("Guest putt noise on short putts" below) and weigh a putt line search only for what break then still costs. Pillar 3.
- Guest putt noise on short putts (found in E3-S24 review). A guest putt's power error is a fixed share of the swing bar (`putt_power_sigma_best` 4.5% to `putt_power_sigma_worst` 12%, `guests/guest_hole.gd`) with no floor, so a 1 to 2 yd tap-in gets the same yard error as a long putt. E3-S24 measured (`/balance reactions --runs 50 --seed 1`): on the flat green from 10 and 15 yd skills 1/4/7/10 three-putt 61/53/36/19% (mean 3.50/2.88/2.30/1.81 putts); putts from 2 yd or less are holed 34 to 52% at skills 1 to 4 and 77 to 84% at skill 10 on every green. A typical guest who misses half their tap-ins and three-putts most flat greens will look broken in the feed and the map, and E4's reactions and rating inherit it (challenge and pace, E3-S8). Proposed: scale putt power noise with putt length (or cap it on short putts), then retune `putt_power_sigma_*` and `putt_aim_sigma_*` with `/balance reactions` against a stated short-putt or three-putt target, keeping E3-S8's targets, or record why not. A change to the noise model needs a `/decision`. Pillar 3.
